1 Introduction / 引言

                                                             Introduction
測圓海鏡簡介
                                                             The Ceyuan Haijing (測圓海鏡, literally “Sea Mirror of Cir-
《測圓海鏡》乃李冶（李冶，1192–1279）之傑作。李冶為
                                                             cle Measurements”) is the masterpiece of Li Ye (李冶, also
 宋元四大數學家之一，與楊輝、秦九韶、朱世傑齊名。
                                                             known as Li Zhi, 1192–1279), one of the four great mathe-
 此書成於 1248 年，系統運用天元術（多項式代數方法）
                                                             maticians of the Song–Yuan period of China, alongside Yang
 求解圓與直角三角形相關之一百七十問。
                                                             Hui, Qin Jiushao, and Zhu Shijie.
 全書結構如下：
                                                             Completed in 1248, this work systematically applies the
 • 卷一：理論基礎——圓城圖式、總率名號、今問正數、                                  tian yuan shu (天元術, “method of the celestial element”)—
   識別雜記（含六百九十二條幾何公式）                                         an algebraic method for setting up and solving polynomial
                                                             equations—to a total of 170 geometric problems concerning
 • 卷二至卷十二：一百七十問，以天元術求解，方程次
                                                             a circle inscribed in or related to a right triangle (勾股容
   數自一次至六次。
                                                             圓).
本文檔包含卷一至卷三之全文，即全部基礎理論及首批                                     The book is organized as follows:
問題。
                                                              • Volume 1 establishes the theoretical foundation: the
                                                                Circle City Diagram (圓城圖式), the General Rate Names
                                                                (總率名號), the Current Question Numbers (今問正數),
                                                                and the Identification Miscellaneous Records (識別雜記)
                                                                containing 692 geometric formulas.
                                                              • Volumes 2–12 present 170 problems, solved by the tian
                                                                yuan shu method. The problems involve equations of
                                                                degrees ranging from 1 to 6.
                                                             The present document contains the complete text of Vol-
                                                             umes 1–3, comprising all foundational material and the first
                                                             suite of problems.

2 Original Preface / 原序

                                                             Original Preface
原序
                                                                 Numbers are fundamentally difficult to ex-
      數本難窮，吾欲以力強窮之，彼其數不惟不                                        haust; if I try to force them by effort, not only
      能得其凡，而吾之力且憊矣。然則數果不可                                        will I fail to apprehend their essence, but my
      以窮耶？既已名之數矣，則又何為而不可窮                                        strength will be depleted as well. Yet can num-
      也。                                                         bers truly not be exhausted? Since they are
                                                                 already called numbers, why then could they
      故謂數為難窮斯可，謂數為不可窮斯不可。                                        not be exhausted?
      何則？彼其冥冥之中，固有昭昭者存，夫昭
      昭者其自然之數也。非自然之數，其自然之                                        It is acceptable to say that numbers are diffi-
      理也。數一出於自然，吾欲以力強窮之，使                                        cult to exhaust; but it is unacceptable to say
      隸首復生亦末如之何也已。                                               they cannot be exhausted. Why? In the midst
                                                                 of darkness, there is indeed brightness. That
      苟能推自然之理，以明自然之數，則雖遠而                                        brightness is the natural number. It is not
      乾端坤倪，幽而神情鬼狀，未有不合者矣。                                        merely the natural number—it is the natural
                                                                 principle. Since numbers arise from nature, if
                                                                 I try to force them by effort, even if Li Shou
                                                                 (the legendary inventor of arithmetic) were
                                                                 reborn, he could do no better.

                                                                 If one can extend natural principles to illumi-
                                                                 nate natural numbers, then even things as dis-
                                                                 tant as the corners of heaven and earth, or as
                                                                 mysterious as spirits and ghosts, will all be in
                                                                 agreement.

      予自幼喜算數，恆病夫考圓之術例出於牽                                                Since youth I have delighted in arithmetic.
      強，殊乖於自然，如古率、徽率、密率之不                                               I was always troubled that the methods for
      同，截弧、截矢、截背之互見，內外諸角，                                               investigating circles seemed forced and ar-
      析剖支條，莫不各自名家，與世作法。及反                                               tificial, contrary to nature—the ancient rate,
      覆研究，卒無以當吾心焉。                                                      Hui’s rate, and the close rate all differing;
                                                                        the arc, sagitta, and back each being consid-
      老大以來，得洞淵九容之說，日夕玩繹，而                                               ered separately; the internal and external an-
      向之病我者，使爆然落去而無遺余。山中多                                               gles analyzed into minute subdivisions—each
      暇，客有從余求其說者，於是乎又為衍之，                                               school establishing its own name and method
      遂累一百七十問。                                                          for the world. After repeated study, none
                                                                        could satisfy my mind.
      既成編，客復目之《測圓海鏡》，蓋取夫天
      臨海鏡之義也。                                                           In old age, I obtained the Dongyuan Nine Con-
                                                                        tainments (洞淵九容) theory, and pondered
                                                                        it day and night. What had previously trou-
                                                                        bled me suddenly fell away completely, leav-
                                                                        ing no trace. With leisure time in the moun-
                                                                        tains, when guests asked me to explain this
                                                                        theory, I expanded upon it, eventually accu-
                                                                        mulating one hundred and seventy problems.

                                                                        When the compilation was complete, a guest
                                                                        named it Ceyuan Haijing—taking the meaning
                                                                        of “heaven overlooking the sea mirror.”
測圓海鏡—Sea Mirror of Circle Measurements (Bilingual Edition)                                                4


      昔半山老人集《唐百家詩選》，自謂廢日力                                    In the past, the Old Man of Banshan compiled
      於此，良可惜。明道先生以上蔡謝君記誦為                                    the Anthology of One Hundred Tang Poets, say-
      玩物喪志。夫文史尚矣，猶之為不足貴，況                                    ing that to waste his days on such a thing was
      九九賤技能乎！                                                truly a pity. Master Mingdao criticized Xie
                                                             of Shangcai for memorizing texts as “playing
      嗜好酸鹹，平生每痛自戒勅，竟莫能已，類                                    with things and losing one’s ambition.” Lit-
      有物慿之者，吾亦不知其然而然也。                                       erature and history are esteemed subjects, yet
                                                             even they are considered not worth valuing—
      故嘗私為之解曰：由技兼於事者言之，夷之                                    how much less the lowly skill of multiplica-
      禮，夔之樂，亦不免為一技。由技進乎道者                                    tion!
      言之，石之斤，扁之輪，非聖人之所與乎。
                                                             My fondness for such sour and salty pursuits—
      覽吾之編，察吾苦心，其憫我者當百數，其                                    throughout my life I have sternly admonished
      笑我者當千數。乃若吾之所自得，則自得焉                                    myself against them, yet in the end I could not
      耳，寧復為人憫笑計哉。                                            stop, as if some force were driving me. I my-
                                                             self do not know why it is so.
                                        李冶序
                                                             So I have privately explained it thus: speaking
                                                             from the perspective of skills combined with
                                                             affairs, even the rites of Yi and the music of
                                                             Kui were not exempt from being a single skill.
                                                             Speaking from the perspective of advancing
                                                             from skill to the Way, was not the stone-axe of
                                                             [the sage] and the wheel of Bian [the wheel-
                                                             wright] praised by the sages?

                                                             Those who read my compilation and perceive
                                                             my earnest efforts—those who pity me will
                                                             number in the hundreds, those who laugh at
                                                             me will number in the thousands. As for what
                                                             I myself have gained, I have simply gained it;
                                                             how could I calculate for the sake of being
                                                             pitied or laughed at?

                                                                                         — Preface by Li Ye

3.1 Circle City Diagram / 圓城圖式
圓城圖式為一百七十問所基之主幾何圖形。其構為一大                                     The Circle City Diagram is the master geometric configura-
直角三角形（勾股形）
         ，內切一圓，並設特定之點與線。                                     tion upon which all 170 problems are based. It consists of a
                                                             large right triangle (called 勾股形, “leg–leg shape”) with its
其情境如下：                                                       inscribed circle, together with specific points and lines.
      假令有圓城一所，不知周徑，四面開門，門                                    The scenario Li Ye constructs is:
      外縱橫各有十字大道。其西北十字道頭定
      為乾地，其東北十字道頭定為艮地，其東南                                          Suppose there is a circular city whose circum-
      十字道頭定為巽地，其西南十字道頭定為                                           ference and diameter are unknown. It has
      坤地。所有測望雜法一一設問如後。                                             gates on all four sides, and outside each gate
                                                                   there are crossroads running north–south and
                                                                   east–west. The northwest crossroad is desig-
                                                                   nated Qian (乾), the northeast Gen (艮), the
                                                                   southeast Xun (巽), and the southwest Kun
                                                                   (坤). All surveying methods are set forth as
                                                                   questions below.

二十二名點 / The 22 Named Points
最大直角三角形之頂點為天、地、乾。內切圓之心稱心。                                    The largest right triangle has vertices Tian (天, Heaven), Di
各點以單字命名：                                                     (地, Earth), and Qian (乾). The center of the inscribed cir-
                                                             cle is called Xin (心, Heart). Key points include intersec-
        天    地    乾    心    日   南    北    川                  tions of horizontal and vertical lines through the center and
        東    西    艮    坤    山   月    巽    青                  other constructed lines. Each point is designated by a single
        泉    朱    金    泛    旦   夕                            Chinese character:


                                                              Tian     Di     Qian    Xin    Ri       Nan    Bei    Chuan
                                                              Dong     Xi     Gen     Kun    Shan     Yue    Xun    Qing
                                                              Quan     Zhu    Jin     Fan    Dan      Xi


3.2 General Rate Names / 總率名號
 全書研究十五個直角三角形，皆以天地線之一段為弦                                     The work studies 15 right triangles, all having a segment of
（弦，即斜邊）。總率名號定義各三角形之三邊。                                       the Tiandi (天地) line as their hypotenuse (弦, “string”). The
 每個三角形：                                                      General Rate Names define these triangles and their three
                                                             sides.
 • 弦（c）——斜邊
                                                             In each triangle:
 • 股（b）——較長之直邊
                                                              • 弦 (c) — the hypotenuse (斜邊)
 • 勾（a）——較短之直邊
                                                              • 股 (b) — the longer vertical leg
                                                              • 勾 (a) — the shorter horizontal leg

 • 凡大差小差相乘為半段徑冪。                                             • The product of the large difference and small difference
                                                               equals half the square of the diameter.
 • 大差勾小差股相乘同上。
                                                             • The product of the large-difference short-leg and the
 • 黃廣股黃長勾相乘為經冪。

                                                               b = c+d, where d is the diameter of the inscribed circle.

4 Volume 2 / 卷二

                                                             Fourteen Questions on Standard Rates
正率一十四問
                                                             These fourteen problems form the “Dongyuan Nine Con-
 此十四問構成「洞淵九容」序列，源自道教大師李思聰
                                                             tainments” (洞淵九容) sequence, derived from the earlier
（號洞淵）之著作。其呈現直角三角形中九種基本容圓類
                                                             work of the Daoist master Li Sicong (李 思 聰, known as
 型，另加五題。
                                                             “Master Dongyuan”). They present the nine fundamental
                                                             types of circles contained in or related to a right triangle,
                                                             plus five additional problems.



4.0.1 第一問 / Problem 1
問 甲乙二人俱在乾地，乙東行三百二十步而立，甲南行                                    Problem. Two people, A and B, both stand at Qian. B walks
六百步望見乙，問徑幾里？                                                 320 steps east and stops. A walks 600 steps south and sees
                                                             B. What is the diameter?
答 城徑二百四十步。
                                                             Answer. The city diameter is 240 steps.
術 此為勾股容圓也。以勾股相乘，倍之為實，並勾股冪
以求弦，復加入勾股共，以為法。                                              Method. This is the “circle contained in the right trian-
                                                             gle” case. Multiply the two legs and double it to obtain the
草 置甲南行六百步在地，以乙東行三百二十步乘之，得
                                                             dividend. Combine the squares of the legs to find the hy-
一十九萬二千步，倍之得三十入萬四千步為實。以乙東
                                                             potenuse, then add the sum of the legs to form the divisor.
行步自之，得一十萬零二千四百步為勾冪；以甲南行步
自之，得三十六萬步為股冪。二冪相並，得四十六萬二                                     Working. Place A’s 600 steps south. Multiply by B’s 320
千四百步為方實，以平方開之，得六百八十步，則弦也。                                    steps east, obtaining 192,000 steps; double to get 384,000 as

                                                             tiply the two legs and double as dividend. Use the sum of

三萬八千二百四十步為實。置乙東行自之，得三萬六千
八百六十四步為勾冪；又置甲南行自之，得一十二萬九                                     Working. Multiply the two walking distances: 69,120; dou-
千六百步為股冪。二冪相並，得一十六萬六千四百六十                                     ble to get 138,240 as dividend. Square B’s eastward steps:
四步為方實，以平方開之，得四百零八，即弦也。又置                                     36,864 as the short-leg square. Square A’s southward steps:

〔按〕此題用勾股求得弦，即可加減得弦較較為城徑。今
 必以勾股相乘倍積為實，求得弦較和為法，而後始得弦                                    [Explanatory note: This problem uses the right triangle to find
 較較為城徑者，蓋欲因此並明勾股相乘之倍積為弦較較、                                   the hypotenuse, then by addition and subtraction obtains the
 弦較和相乘之積，非故為紆回也。                                             “hypotenuse-difference-difference” as the city diameter. The
                                                             method deliberately uses the product of the legs (doubled) as
                                                             dividend to demonstrate that this product equals the product
                                                             of (hypotenuse−difference) and (hypotenuse+difference).]

5.0.1 第一問 / Problem 1
問 乙出東門南行，不知步數而止。甲出西門南行四百八                                    Problem. B goes out the East Gate and walks south, an un-

草 識別得二行相減，餘三十步，即乙出東門南行步也。
                                                             tances; multiply by twice A’s southward steps to obtain the
倍相減步，得六十步，以乘二之甲南行步九百六十步，
                                                             square-root dividend; this gives the city diameter.
得五萬七千六百步，為平方實。如法開之，得二百四十
步，即城徑也。合問。                                                   Working. Recognize: subtracting the two distances leaves
                                                             30 steps, which is B’s southward distance from the East
                                                             Gate. Double the difference: 60 steps. Multiply by twice
                                                             A’s southward steps (960): 57,600, the square-root dividend.

 一之數乎？故曰不受也。寄分者，姑寄其應除之數也。
                                                             Then square the celestial element diameter and multiply by
 俟求得兩相等數，而此數內尚少一除，不除此而轉乘彼，
                                                             the middle short-leg as the equal number; cancel with the
 則兩數仍相等，猶之受除者也。此所謂以乘代除也。
                                                             left; extract the cube root with a following edge: 240 steps,
                                                             the city diameter. This agrees.
                                                             [Explanatory note: “Not accepted for division” means there is
                                                             no valid way to divide. When two numbers are such that one
                                                             can be divided by the other, the division is “accepted”; other-
                                                             wise it is “not accepted.” For division has divisor and dividend;
                                                             the dividend can be compound, but the divisor cannot. Here
                                                             the middle short-leg serves as divisor, but it contains both the
                                                             celestial element and 16 steps—it is already compound. How
                                                             can one evenly divide a non-uniform number? Hence “not ac-
                                                             cepted.” Storing the denominator means temporarily retaining
                                                             the number that should be divided. When two equal quanti-
                                                             ties are found, and one still lacks a division, instead of dividing
                                                             this, multiply that; the two quantities remain equal, as if the
                                                             division had been accepted. This is the so-called “substituting
                                                             multiplication for division.”]
測圓海鏡—Sea Mirror of Circle Measurements (Bilingual Edition)                                                              16

6 The Tian Yuan Shu Method / 天元術

                                                             The Tian Yuan Shu Method
天元術
                                                             The tian yuan shu (天 元 術, “method of the celestial ele-
 天元術（
    「天元術」「method of the celestial element」
         ，                               ）為
                                                             ment”) is the algebraic technique employed throughout the
《測圓海鏡》全書所運用之代數技法。此乃中國數學史
                                                             Ceyuan Haijing. It is the earliest systematic method in Chi-
 上最早之系統方法，用以建立並求解一元多項式方程。
                                                             nese mathematics for setting up and solving polynomial
                                                             equations with one unknown.

6.1 Methodology / 方法論
其程序如下：                                                       The procedure follows these steps:
 1. 立天元一為 X（「設天元一為 X」）——宣告未知量。                               1. Set the celestial element unity to be X (“立天元一為
    以現代記號，等同於「令 x = X 」。                                        X ”) — This declares the unknown quantity. In modern
                                                                notation, it is equivalent to “Let x = X.”
 2. 以天元 x 表達所有已知量及關係。
                                                             2. Express all given quantities and relationships in terms
 3. 構造兩個相等之表達式（常以不同方法計算同一幾
                                                                of the celestial element x.
    何量），一為「左」式暫存，另一為「同數」。
                                                             3. Construct two equal expressions (often representing the
 4. 相消（
      「mutual cancellation」
                          ）——令兩式相等並化簡，
                                                                same geometric quantity computed by different meth-
    得多項式方程之標準形式。
                                                                ods), one designated as the “left” (左) expression and
 5. 以開方法求解：開平方、開立方、或更高次之開方。                                     stored temporarily, the other as the “equal number” (同
                                                                數).
                                                             4. Mutual cancellation (相消) — Set the two expressions
                                                                equal and simplify to obtain the polynomial equation in
                                                                standard form.
                                                             5. Solve the equation by root-extraction methods (開方):
                                                                square-root extraction (開 平 方), cube-root extraction
                                                                (開立方), or higher-degree root extraction.

6.2 Polynomial Notation / 多項式記號
李冶運用籌算板上的位置記法：                                               Li Ye uses a positional notation on a counting-rod board:
 • 係數縱向排列，常數項（實）在上。                                           • Coefficients are arranged vertically, with the constant
                                                                term (實) at the top.
 • 其下：x 之係數（方，  「square」
                       ），繼以 x （廉，
                                「edge」
                                     ），2

   繼以 x3 （隅，「corner」）。                                        • Below it: the coefficient of x (方, “square”), then x2 (廉,
                                                                “edge”), then x3 (隅, “corner”).
 • 更高次：第一廉、第二廉等。
                                                              • For higher degrees: first 廉, second 廉, etc.
 • 負係數以末位斜劃表示。
                                                              • Negative coefficients are indicated by a diagonal stroke
                                                                through the last digit.
測圓海鏡—Sea Mirror of Circle Measurements (Bilingual Edition)                                                                18


6.3 Example: Quadratic Equation / 示例：二次方程
第一問導出相當於下式之方程：                                               The first problem leads to the equation equivalent to:

                  384000 = 1600 · d                                               384000 = 1600 · d

其中 d 為直徑。但更複雜之問題產生真正的多項式方程。                                  where d is the diameter. But more complex problems yield
例如卷三第四問產生三次方程：                                               true polynomial equations. For instance, Problem 4 of Vol-
                                                             ume 3 produces a cubic equation:
         (4 · 16)(480)2 = (480 + 16) · d2 − d3
                                                                        (4 · 16)(480)2 = (480 + 16) · d2 − d3
以現代形式，李冶以「帶縱立方」解之。
                                                             in modern form, which Li Ye solves by “opening the cube
                                                             with an edge” (帶縱立方).

                                             大股一十八問・Large Leg

【題意總說】卷五論「大股」之術。所謂大股，乃最大勾股 [General Summary] Volume 5 treats the “large leg” (大
 形之股邊，即通股也。以大股為主，配以諸勾股形之關係， 股) method. The large leg is the leg-side of the largest
 求圓城之徑。凡一十八問。大股者，勾股形中最長之股，即 right triangle—the universal leg. Taking the large leg as
 甲從乾隅直南至坤隅之步數，為六百步之率也。諸問或知 principal, combined with relations among sub-triangles,
 乙南行之數，或知甲南行之數，或知斜望之數，皆以大股 we seek the city diameter. All 18 problems. The large
 為基本建立天元方程。此卷方程多為三次、四次，須開立 leg is A’s full southward walk from the Qian corner to
 方、開三乘方以求之，為天元術之精深處。        the Kun corner—the 600-pace standard. Given B’s south-
                            ward walk, or A’s southward walk, or diagonal sightings,
                            we establish tian yuan equations. This volume features
                            third- and fourth-degree equations requiring cube-root
                            and fourth-root extraction—the subtle core of tian yuan.

【題意總說】卷六論「大勾」之術。所謂大勾，乃最大勾股 [General Summary] Volume 6 treats the “large base”
 形之勾邊，即通勾也。以大勾為主，配以諸勾股形之關係， (大 勾) method. The large base is the base-side of the
 求圓城之徑。凡一十八問。大勾者，甲從乾隅直東至艮隅 largest right triangle—the universal base. Taking the
 之步數，為三百二十步之率也。與大股相對而言，大股為 large base as principal, combined with sub-triangle re-
 南北向，大勾為東西向。此卷之問多設乙從東門直行若干 lations, we seek the city diameter. All 18 problems. The
 步，甲從乾隅東行若干步望乙之狀，以建立天元方程。方 large base is A’s eastward walk from the Qian corner to
 程之次數較高，有三次、四次之方程，須開立方、開三乘 the Gen corner—the 320-pace standard. Contrasted with
 方求之，為全書最精深之卷。              the large leg (north-south), the large base is east-west.
                            This volume mostly posits B walking east from the east
                            gate and A walking east from the Qian corner to view B,
                            establishing tian yuan equations. Equations here reach
                            higher degrees—third and fourth—requiring cube-root
                            and fourth-root extraction. The most profound volume
                            in the entire work.

                                   On choosing the celestial element: Why use diameter
                                   for some problems and radius for others? It depends
                                   on convenience. If conditions relate to the diameter di-
                                   rectly, use diameter; if to the radius, use radius. Differ-
                                   ent choices yield the same result—the flexibility of tian
                                   yuan.

                                               coefficient; four times A’s walk minus twice B’s walk as
                                               corner; four as negative corner; extract fourth root for
                                               the diameter.

附錄：全書結構及術語解釋・Appendix: Structure and Glossary
《測圓海鏡》全書十二卷，共一百七十問，為元代李冶先生 Ceyuan Haijing (Sea Mirror of Circle Measurements), in
 之傑作。其書以勾股測圓為主，設圓城一座，甲乙二人從 twelve volumes and 170 problems, is the masterpiece
 城門出行，以行步之數推求圓徑。全書結構如下：       of Master Li Ye of the Yuan Dynasty. The work uses
                              right-triangle circle measurement, positing a circular
                              city from whose walkers’ paces the diameter is derived.
     卷  篇名    問數     起止問
                              The complete structure:
     卷一 圓城圖式   —      圖說
     卷二 正率     14  第 1–14 問
     卷三 邊股     17  第 15–31 問          Vol. Title            Probs.    Range
     卷四 底勾     17  第 32–48 問            1   City Diagram      —      Diagram
     卷五 大股     18  第 49–66 問            2   Standard Rates    14       1–14
     卷六 大勾     18  第 67–84 問            3   Side Leg          17       15–31
     卷七 明勾     16 第 85–100 問            4   Base Leg          17       32–48
     卷八 明股     16 第 101–126 問           5   Large Leg         18       49–66
     卷九 雜題上    18 第 127–144 問           6   Large Base        18       67–84
     卷十 雜題中    18 第 145–162 問           7   Bright Base       16      85–100
    卷十一 雜題下    18 第 163–180 問           8   Bright Leg        16     101–126
    卷十二 雜題終    10 第 181–190 問           9   Misc. Part I      18     127–144
        合計    170                      10   Misc. Part II     18     145–162
                                       11   Misc. Part III    18     163–180
                                       12   Misc. Finale      10     181–190
                                                    Total          170

術語解釋・Glossary of Terms:                     English Glossary:
• 天元一：設未知數為一，即現代代數之 x。李冶先生以 • Tian yuan yi (天元一): The celestial element unity; the
 「立天元一」為某數，即設未知數為所求之量。           unknown x. “Let tian yuan unity be [quantity]” means:
                                 let x equal the sought quantity.
• 寄左：將某式暫存一邊，相當於方程之一邊。天元術中
  常以「寄左」表示方程之左邊，後以「同數」與之相消。 • Ji zuo (寄左): Deposit on the left; temporarily store an
                                 expression on one side of the equation. The “left” side,
• 相消：兩式相減，相當於移項合併。天元術之核心步驟，
                                 later canceled with the “equal number.”
  以同數與寄左之式相減，得開方式。
                               • Xiang xiao (相消): Mutual cancellation; subtracting
• 帶分母：某式含有分母未除。古代算術中常「不受除」，
                                 two expressions, equivalent to transposition and com-
  即以帶分母之形式保留，以待後續運算。
                                 bining terms. The core step of tian yuan.
• 冪：平方，即現代記號 x2 。天元術中以某數自乘為其冪。
                               • Dai fen mu (帶分母): Carrying a denominator; an ex-
• 開方：開平方，即求平方根。天元術中最基本之運算。       pression with an uncanceled denominator, retained
                                 for later operations.
• 開立方：開立方，即求立方根。用於三次天元方程。
                               • Mi (冪): Power; square, i.e., x2 in modern notation.
• 開三乘方：開四次方，即求四次根。用於四次天元方程，
  為天元術中最高深之運算。                 • Kai fang (開方): Root extraction; square-root extrac-
                                 tion, the fundamental operation of tian yuan.
• 實：常數項，即方程中之已知數部分。
                               • Kai li fang (開立方): Cube-root extraction; for cubic
• 從：一次項係數，天元術中「從法」即 x 之係數。
                                 equations.
• 廉：二次項係數，即 x2 之係數。
                               • Kai san cheng fang (開 三 乘 方): Fourth-root extrac-
• 隅：三次項係數（立方方程中），即 x3 之係數。四次方    tion; for quartic equations—the most advanced opera-
  程中另有「三乘隅」或「益隅」之稱。              tion in tian yuan.
• 勾：直角三角形之短邊（底邊）。                           • Shi (實): Dividend; the constant term of the equation.
• 股：直角三角形之長邊（高邊）。                           • Cong (從): Coefficient; the linear term coefficient (x
                                              term).
• 弦：直角三角形之斜邊。
                                            • Lian (廉): Corner; the quadratic term coefficient (x2
• 大勾：最大勾股形之勾邊，即通勾。
                                              term).
• 大股：最大勾股形之股邊，即通股。
                                            • Yu (隅): Corner divisor; the cubic term coefficient (x3
• 大差：大股減圓徑之餘數。                                term). In quartics: “triple-multiplication corner” or
                                              “negative corner.”
• 小差：小勾加半徑之和。
                           • Gou (勾): Base; the shorter leg of a right triangle.
• 底勾：勾股形之最下勾股，與圓徑相切之基本勾股形之
  勾邊。                      • Gu (股): Leg; the longer leg of a right triangle.
                                            • Xian (弦): Hypotenuse; the diagonal side.
                                            • Da gou (大勾): Large base; the universal base.
                                            • Da gu (大股): Large leg; the universal leg.
                                            • Da cha (大差): Large difference; large leg minus diam-
                                              eter.
                                            • Xiao cha (小差): Small difference; small base plus ra-
                                              dius.
                                            • Di gou (底勾): Base leg; the lowest triangle tangent to
                                              the circle.

天元術方程式記法・Tian Yuan Equation Notation:                    Tian Yuan Equation Notation:
李冶先生以籌算式記多項式，自下而上排列：                                     Master Li Ye recorded polynomials in counting-rod style,
• 最下為常數項（實）                                              arranged bottom-to-top:
• 其上為一次項（從）                                              • Lowest: constant term (dividend, shí)
• 再其上為二次項（廉）                                             • Above: linear term (coefficient, cóng)
• 再其上為三次項（隅）                             • Above that: quadratic term (corner, lián)
                   ， 0 0 48000 表示 48000− • Above that: cubic term (corner divisor, yú)
例如 480 表示 480−x（一次式）
     1                 1 0 0
                                         Thus 480 denotes 480 − x (linear), and 0 0 48000 de-
x2 之類。其中係數為負者以斜線或特殊記號表示之。                        1                                      1 0 0
                                         notes 48000 − x2 . Negative coefficients were indicated by
                                         slanted lines or special marks.

                             Volume               Quadratic Cubic Quartic
 卷             平方（二次）三乘方（四次）   立方（三次）

 卷五（大股）  6問     10 問    2 問 Vol. 6 (Large Base)       3          6       9
 卷六（大勾）  3問     6問      9問
                           As the table shows, equation degrees increase toward
由上表可見，愈至卷六，方程之次數愈高，開方之術愈繁， Volume 6, and root- extraction methods become more
乃李冶先生循序漸進、由淺入深之教學法也。       complex. This is Master Li Ye’s pedagogical method:
                           gradual progression from simple to profound.

1 緒論                                                                                Introduction

   測圓海鏡者，金元之際李冶潤甫先生之所著                        The Ceyuan Haijing (Sea Mirror of Circle Measure-
也。先生諱治，字仁卿，號敬齋，真定欒城人，生於金                 ments) was written by Li Ye (Li Zhi, 1192–1279) during
明昌元年（一一九二），卒於元至元十六年（一二七                  the Jin-Yuan transition period. A native of Luancheng,
九）。此書成於淳祐八年（一二四八），凡十二卷，計                 Zhending, he completed this work in 1248. The book
一百七十問，皆設圓城以為問，用天元術以立算，實                  comprises 12 volumes with 170 problems, all concern-
中算之鴻篇也。                                  ing a circular walled city, solved by the tian yuan shu
                                         (heavenly element method)—a masterpiece of Chinese
                                         mathematics.
  其法之要，在於天元一術。立天元一為所求之                         The essence of the method lies in the tian yuan
數，依題意布算，得開方式，然後開方求之。所謂太                  technique: set the celestial unknown as the quantity
極、天元、地元者，猶今代數之未知數也。書中凡遇                  sought, derive a polynomial equation (the “opening pat-
開方式，皆以 ■ 記之，蓋當時算板布列之位也。                  tern”) according to the problem conditions, then extract
                                         the root. The terms Taiji, Tian Yuan, and Di Yuan cor-
                                         respond to algebraic unknowns. The symbol ■ marks
                                         positions on the counting board where polynomial coef-
                                         ficients were arranged.
   卷七至卷九所載，凡三十四問，其題目之繁，方                       Volumes 7–9 contain 34 problems of greater com-
程式次之高，皆過於前六卷。卷七曰「明前一十八                   plexity than the first six volumes, with equations reach-
問」，卷八曰「明後一十六問」，卷九曰「大斜四問、大                ing the 4th–6th degree. Volume 7 treats “front” con-
和八問」。其問題之設，或二人對行，或四處遙望，或                 figurations, Volume 8 “rear” configurations, and Vol-
穿城而過，或倚樹而參，錯綜變化， 不可勝窮。                   ume 9 the most challenging “oblique” and “sum” prob-
                                         lems. The scenarios involve pairs or groups of observers,
                                         tree landmarks, and gates around the circular city.

體例說明                                     Structure of Each Problem
    每問之體，例分三節：                                Each problem follows a standard format:

  • 或問：設問之辭，述人物行止之狀、所給之數。                   • Q (或問): The problem statement, describing the
                                              scene and given quantities.
  • 法曰：列方程式之術，示開方之次。
                                            • Method (法曰): The polynomial setup and root-
  • 草曰：詳布算之草，盡天元變化之妙。                         extraction procedure.
又有時設答曰一節，直書所求之數，
               以為開方之驗。                      • Working (草曰): Detailed step-by-step derivation
                                              using tian yuan.

                                         An Answer (答曰) section sometimes states the result
                                         directly as verification.

  書中所用十五形之名，皆有所本。通勾、通股                        The text uses fifteen named right triangles. Tong
者，大勾股形之兩邊也；邊勾、邊股者，邊上之小形                  (通) gou/gu are the legs of the main large triangle. Bian
也；底勾、底股者，底下之小形也。明勾、明股者，明                 (邊) and Di (底) gou/gu belong to smaller edge/base
朗易見之小形也；重勾、重股者，重疊相因之小形                   triangles. Ming (明, “bright”) and Zhong (重, “over-
也。至於虛勾、虛股、虛弦，則太虛之形，最為微妙。                 lapping”) triangles are visually apparent. The Xu (虛,
                                         “void”) triangle is the most subtle—the inner void space.
                                         Each problem typically asks for the city diameter (城
                                         徑): 240 paces.




                           Li Ye (李冶) — Volumes 7–9
Ceyuan Haijing Bilingual                                                                            3



2 卷七         明前一十八問                                                     Vol. 7: 18 “Front” Problems

卷七小序：此卷一十八問，皆以明段為主。所謂明段                  Volume 7 Preface: These 18 problems all concern “bright”
者，勾股形之顯然可見者也。其題多設二人對行、遙                  (ming) segments—the visibly apparent right-triangle edges.
望相會之狀，或倚門傍樹，或穿城越隍，錯綜參伍，                  Scenarios involve pairs walking toward each other, gaz-
以盡天元之變。其方程式之高，有至三乘、四乘者，                  ing from afar, leaning by gates or trees, passing through
開方之法亦隨之益密。                               the city. The equations reach degrees 3–4, with increas-
                                         ingly refined root-extraction techniques.


第一百零五問                                                                         Problem 一百零五
或問：出南門東行七十二步有樹，出東門南行三十                   Q: Going out the South Gate and walking 72 paces east
步見之。問答同前。                                there is a tree. Going out the East Gate and walking 30
                                         paces south, one sees it. Answer as before.
答曰：
  城徑二百四十步，半徑一百二十步。                       A: City diameter: 240 paces. Radius: 120 paces.
法曰：倍南行以乘倍東行為平實，並二行又倍之為                   Method: Double the southward walk, multiply by dou-
從，一虛隅。得城徑。                               ble the eastward walk as the flat shi. Add the two
                                         walks and double as cong. One empty corner. Extract
                                         the city diameter.
草曰：識別得此問名為弦外容圓，又為內率求虛積。                  Working: Identification: This problem is “circle contained
其二行步相並為虛弦，若以相減即虛較也。又倍東                   outside the hypotenuse” and also “seeking the void area
行為弦較和，倍南行即弦較較，此二數相乘則兩虛                   by inner rate.” The sum of the two walks is the void hy-
                                         potenuse; their difference is the void difference. Double
積也。若直以二行相乘，則半個虛積也。又倍東行減
                                         the eastward walk gives the sum of hypotenuse and dif-
於城徑，餘即二虛勾也。倍南行減於城徑則二虛股                   ference; double the southward walk gives their difference.
也。虛積上三事和即城徑也。                            Their product is twice the void area; direct multiplication
  乃立天元一為圓徑，便以為三事和也。倍二行                   gives half the void area. The city diameter minus double
步減之，得 □ 為黃方一，天元乘之得 □ 為二虛                 the eastward walk gives twice the void gou; minus double
                                         the southward walk gives twice the void gu. The three-
積（寄左）。然後倍東行以乘倍南行，得八千六百四
                                         element sum on the void area equals the city diameter.
十為同數，與左相消得 □ 。益積開平方得二百四
十步，即城徑也。合問。                                    Let the celestial unknown be the circle diameter, as
                                         the three-element sum. Subtracting double the two walks
                                         gives ■ as the yellow square; multiplying by the unknown
                                         gives ■ as twice the void area (stored left). Then double the
                                         eastward walk times double the southward walk gives 8640
                                         as the matching number. Canceling gives ■. Accumulating
                                         and extracting the square root yields 240 paces, the city
                                         diameter. Q.E.D.

自之，減上位為平方實，二之出南門東行步為益從，                  upper term. Square the East Gate walk and subtract
一步常法。翻開得半徑。                              from upper term as the quadratic shi. Double the
                                         south-gate eastward walk as increasing cong. One
                                         constant method. Extract by reversing to obtain the

                                         radius.

徑，加一十六減七十二，得 □ 為勾也。以自之得                  72 paces east is the difference between level gou and level

第一百一十問                                                                         Problem 一百一十

2.1 卷八 明後一十六問                                                            Vol. 8: 16 “Rear” Problems

卷八小序：此卷一十六問，承卷七之緒，仍以明段為                  Volume 8 Preface: These 16 problems continue from
主，而益之以極段、虛段之變。所謂極段者，皇極勾                  Volume 7, still centered on “bright” segments but now
股形之各邊也；虛段者，太虛勾股形之各邊也。此三                  incorporating “extreme” and “void” variations. The ex-
形交錯互見，開方之術益臻精妙。其方程式之高，有                  treme segments are the edges of the imperial extreme
至四乘方、五乘方者，實全書之樞紐也。                       right triangle; the void segments are edges of the grand
                                         void triangle. These three triangles interweave, and the
                                         root-extraction techniques reach their finest. Equations
                                         rise to degrees 4–5; this is the pivot of the entire work.

2.2 卷九 大斜四問、大和八問                                              Vol. 9: 4 Oblique + 8 Sum Problems

卷九小序：此卷凡十二問，分為二節。上節曰「大斜                  Volume 9 Preface: These 12 problems divide into two
四問」，以通勾股形之斜邊為主，方程式之高有至                   sections. The upper “Great Oblique” section uses the hy-
五乘、六乘者，實全書開方之極致。下節曰「大和八                  potenuse of the general right triangle, with equations
問」，以通勾股形之三事和為主，其術益奧，其變益                  reaching degrees 5–6—the pinnacle of root extraction in
奇。學者能精於此，則天元之術思過半矣。                      the entire work. The lower “Great Sum” section uses the
                                         three-element sum of the general triangle; the methods
                                         are ever more profound, the variations ever more mar-
                                         velous. Mastery of this volume means more than half
                                         the tian yuan technique is understood.

第一百四十三問                                                                    Problem 一百四十三

  城徑二百四十步。                               A: City diameter: 240 paces.

                                         Geng-Ren sum; square and subtract from upper term

3 跋                                                                                   Afterword

  測圓海鏡一書，自序雲「積思惑悟」，蓋先生潛                        The Ceyuan Haijing, as Li Ye writes in his pref-
心二十餘年而後成之者也。其術以天元一為宗，太                   ace, arose from “accumulated reflection and awakened
極為本，立一未知數而萬端可推，實開中國代數學                   insight”—the product of more than twenty years of de-
之先河。全書一百七十問，無一問不備問、答、術、草                 voted study. Its method takes the celestial unknown as
四體，條理密察，邏輯謹嚴，雖古希臘之幾何原本，                  master and the supreme polarity as foundation: estab-
其精密不過如此。                                 lishing one unknown from which myriad deductions fol-
                                         low, it opened the floodgates of Chinese algebra. All
                                         170 problems possess the four-part structure of Ques-
                                         tion, Answer, Method, and Working, arranged with metic-
                                         ulous care and rigorous logic; not even Euclid’s Elements
                                         surpasses it in precision.
  卷七至卷九，計三十四問，方程式之高有至五                        Volumes 7–9 contain 34 problems with equations
乘、六乘者。其開方之術，或益積，或翻法，或倒積，                 reaching the 5th and 6th degrees. The root-extraction
或消息，變化萬端，而歸於一理。先生又創立十五勾                  techniques—accumulating, reversing, inverting, and adjusting—
股形之說，以通勾股為母，明勾股、極勾股、虛勾股                  vary endlessly yet converge on a single principle. Li Ye
為子，母子相生，條理粲然。此實中國數學史上之偉                  also created the doctrine of fifteen right triangles: the
大創造也。                                    general triangle as parent, with bright, extreme, and
                                         void triangles as children, each generating the other in
                                         crystalline order. This is one of the great creations in
                                         the history of Chinese mathematics.
   後之學者，如元之朱世傑、郭守敬，明之唐順                       Later scholars—Zhu Shijie and Guo Shoujing of
 之、顧應祥，清之李善蘭、華蘅芳，皆深受此書之影                 the Yuan, Tang Shunzhi and Gu Yingxiang of the Ming,
 響。李善蘭譯西算時，猶以天元術與代數相發明，謂                 Li Shanlan and Hua Hengfang of the Qing—all drank
「中國之天元，即西國之代數」，其說誠不我欺也。                  deeply from this source. When Li Shanlan translated
  嗚呼！七百餘年過去，算板之制已湮，天元之術                  Western mathematics, he still used tian yuan to illumi-
猶存。讀此書者，不獨見中算之精深，亦可感先賢之                  nate algebra, declaring “China’s celestial unknown is
睿智。謹識數語，以為後來之勸云。                         the West’s algebra”—a truth that does not deceive us.
                                               Alas! Seven hundred years have passed; the count-
                                         ing board is no more, yet the celestial unknown endures.
                                         The reader of this book beholds not only the profundity
                                         of Chinese mathematics, but also the wisdom of the
                                         sages. These few words are inscribed as encouragement
                                         for those who follow.




                           Li Ye (李冶) — Volumes 7–9

Colophon

    測圓海鏡   （Ceyuan Haijing / Sea Mirror of Circle         The Ceyuan Haijing (Sea Mirror of Circle Measure-
Measurements）為金元數學家李冶（一一九二—一                        ments) was written by the Jin-Yuan mathematician Li
二七九）所著，成書於一二四八年。全書十二卷，一                             Ye (1192–1279) and completed in 1248. The work com-
百七十問，以天元術為核心，開中國代數學之先河。                             prises 12 volumes with 170 problems, centered on the
書中創立十五勾股形之說，設圓城以為問，錯綜變                              tian yuan algebraic method that pioneered Chinese al-
化，極盡天元之妙。此英漢對照本，左列原文，右附                             gebra. Li Ye created a system of fifteen right triangles
英譯，俾中外讀者俱得窺其堂奧。術語對照，力求精                             arranged around a circular city, with intricate varia-
確；草曰翻譯，詳盡無遺。問答術草四體俱存，以存                             tions that exhaust the subtleties of the celestial unknown.
古人之遺法云。                                                   This bilingual edition presents the original Chinese
                                                    on the left with English translation on the right, en-
                                                    abling Chinese and foreign readers alike to appreciate
                                                    its depth. Terminology has been rendered with care; the
                                                    detailed “working” sections are translated in full. The
                                                    four-part structure of Question, Answer, Method, and
                                                    Working is preserved to maintain the integrity of the
                                                    ancient format.

     帶分        With denominator                                Dai Fen        With denominator

 緒論        (Introduction)

    測圓海鏡（Ce-yuan Haijing）乃金元數學家李                       The Sea Mirror of Circle Measurements (Ceyuan Hai-
 冶（1192–1279）之曠世巨著，初刻於元定宗三年                        jing) is the magnum opus of the Jin-Yuan dynasty mathe-
（1248 年），重修於元憲宗九年（1259 年）    。全書十二                 matician Li Ye (1192–1279), first published in 1248 CE and
 卷，共一百七十問，皆圍繞「勾股容圓」之單一幾                            revised in 1259 CE. The complete work comprises twelve
 何構型，以天元術（tian yuan shu，celestial element          volumes and one hundred seventy problems, all centered
 method）推演至極致。                                     on the single geometric configuration of “right triangle con-
                                                   taining a circle,” pushed to its utmost limits through the
                                                   tian yuan shu (celestial element method) — the art of al-
                                                   gebra.


   本冊為全書終卷（卷十至卷十二），共四十問     This volume constitutes the final volumes (Volumes
（第百三十一問至第百七十問）。此三卷代表中古代 10–12) of the complete work, comprising forty problems
 數學之巔峰：                 (Problems 131 through 170). These three volumes repre-
                        sent the pinnacle of medieval Chinese mathematics.


    • 卷十（第 131–140 問）：高次方程之極致，五乘方與六乘方。 Vol. 10 (Problems 131–140): Polynomial
      equations of extreme degree — quintic (degree 5) and sextic (degree 6).

    • 卷十一（第 141–150 問）：多圓交套與多人會合，幾何關係錯綜複雜。 Vol. 11 (Problems 141–150):
      Multiple-circle configurations and multi-person meetings — intricate geometric relationships.

    • 卷十二（第 151–170 問）：終極難題與天元術總結，爐火純青。                                 Vol. 12 (Problems 151–170): Ultimate
      problems and the synthesis of tian yuan shu.


   天元術者，李冶所創之代數方法也。其法立天                                Tian yuan shu (Celestial Element Method) is Li Ye’s
 元一為某某（設未知數為 x），依題意布列天元式                           algebraic method. The procedure: “Establish tian yuan
（多項式），通過相消（消元）得開方式（方程），最                           one as [the unknown]” (let x be the unknown), construct
 後開方（解方程）得之。比西方符號代數早逾五百                            tian yuan shi (polynomials) from the problem conditions,
 年。                                                eliminate via xiang xiao (mutual elimination) to obtain the
                                                   kai fang shi (equation to be solved), and finally perform
                                                   kai fang (root extraction). This predates Western symbolic
                                                   algebra by over five centuries.

     Structure of Each Problem / 每問結構：

    • 問 (wen) — Problem statement / 命題陳述

    • 答曰 (da yue) — Numerical answer / 數值答案

    • 法曰 (fa yue) — General method description / 解法概要

    • 草曰 (cao yue) — Detailed working (with tian yuan shu) / 詳細演算

    • 識別得 (shi bie de) — Geometric analysis / 幾何辨析
 Ce-yuan Haijing Bilingual – Vols. 10–12 (Final)                                                               3

（五次方程），乃至六乘方（六次方程）。      nomial degree — quartic (degree 4), quintic (degree 5), and
                         even sextic (degree 6) equations appear throughout.

   李冶於此卷中將天元術之運用推至極致：布式      In this volume Li Ye pushes tian yuan shu to its ex-

                                                      Extracting the square root yields x = 60 bu; doubling
                                                  gives the diameter 120 bu. This answers the question.

卷十一（Volume 11）
             ：多圓交套與多人會合 (Vol. 11: Multiple Circles and Multiple
Unknowns)

  卷十一計十問（第百四十一問至第百五十問），     Volume 11 comprises ten problems (Problems 141–150),
為全書倒數第二卷。此卷之獨特處在於問題配置 the penultimate volume of the entire work. Its distinctive
之多圓交套與多人會合——不再局限於單一圓城之 feature lies in the configuration of multiple intersecting
勾股容圓，而涉及多個圓形之相交、相切，以及三 circles and multi-person meetings — no longer confined
人、四人甚至更多人之行走會合。         to a single circular city with inscribed right triangles, but
                        involving multiple circles intersecting and tangent to one
                        another, with three, four, or more people meeting.

  李冶於此卷中展示了天元術處理複雜幾何關係     In this volume Li Ye demonstrates the astonishing power
之驚人能力。多圓之交套，其幾何約束成倍增加； of tian yuan shu in handling complex geometric relation-
多人之會合，其代數關係縱橫交錯。天元術以一個 ships. The geometric constraints of multiple circle inter-
未知數統攝眾多變量，層層相消，最終歸結為單一 sections increase manifold; the algebraic relations of multi-
之多項式方程。                person meetings interweave in all directions. Yet tian yuan
                       shu unifies all variables under a single unknown, elimi-
                       nates layer by layer, and ultimately reduces everything to
                       a single polynomial equation.

【第百四十一問：三人出四門】                                    [Problem 141: Three Persons Exit Four Gates ]

步。三人各望見對方，問圓徑幾何？                                  the East Gate and walks 150 bu; B exits the South Gate and

                       is the constant divisor. Extract the square root to obtain the

萃於此。                                              this volume Li Ye pushes tian yuan shu to a state of per-

問：假令圓城一座，甲出東門南行，乙出南門東行， Wen (Problem): Suppose a circular city. A exits the East
丙出西門北行，丁出北門西行。只云甲行一百八十 Gate and walks south; B exits the South Gate and walks east;
步，乙行一百二十步，丙行六十步，丁行四十步。 C exits the West Gate and walks north; D exits the North Gate
問圓徑幾何？                  and walks west. Given that A walked 180 bu, B walked 120
                        bu, C walked 60 bu, D walked 40 bu. What is the diameter?
答曰：圓徑一百二十步。                                       Da (Answer): The diameter of the circle is 120 bu.
識別得：識別得：此問四人各出一門，又各轉向， Shi Bie De (Analysis): Analysis: In this problem four people
形成 extbf 四門環通之構型。甲出東門轉南、乙出 each exit one gate and then turn, forming the extbfFour Gates
南門轉東、丙出西門轉北、丁出北門轉西，四人之 Interconnected configuration. A exits East and turns South;
路徑環繞圓城一周，象徵天地之循環。          B exits South and turns East; C exits West and turns North; D
                           exits North and turns West. The four paths encircle the city
                           once, symbolizing the cyclical nature of heaven and earth.

                       is the constant divisor. Extract the square root to obtain the
                       diameter.

【第百五十五問：高次之巔】                                     [Problem 155: The Summit of Higher Degrees ]

                                                  diameter?

識別得：識別得：此問甲行二百四十步、乙行一百 Shi Bie De (Analysis): Analysis: This problem has A’s walk
六十步、斜行二百步，三數皆大。天元式之係數隨 at 240 bu, B’s at 160 bu, and the diagonal at 200 bu — all
之增大，開方運算更為繁複。此為 extbf 高次之巔 large numbers. The coefficients of the tian yuan equation
——表面為二次方程，但若以完整之天元術推演， increase accordingly, making root extraction more complex.
須經多次相消，冪次逐級升高。             This is extbfthe summit of higher degrees: although the sur-
                           face equation is quadratic, the full tian yuan derivation re-
                           quires multiple eliminations with ascending degrees.

                       dividend. The walk difference is the linear term. One is the
                       constant divisor. Extract the square root to obtain the diam-
                       eter.

                                                  fine C2 , and C’s path define C3 . The three circles share the
                                                  city center as a common point, and the city’s radius is the
                                                  common parameter. Tian yuan shu unifies all three circles
                                                  through x without simultaneous equations.

第百七十問：全書終問——天元歸一 / Problem 170: The Final Problem of the Complete Work — Tian Yuan
Returns to One

                                                  — Tian Yuan Returns to One ]

問：假令圓城一座，甲乙二人同立於圓心。甲向東 Wen (Problem): Suppose a circular city. A and B stand at

即 extbf 最終一問。其特殊之處在於條件「extbf 斜 the entire work, the extbffinal problem. Its unique feature is
行與圓徑等」——斜行之長度恰等於圓徑。此條件 the condition “ extbfthe diagonal equals the diameter” — the
將斜行（弦）與圓徑（直徑）等同起來，象徵 extbf diagonal’s length is exactly equal to the diameter. This con-
天元歸一——曲線之弦與圓之直徑相等，曲直合一， dition equates the diagonal (the chord of a curve) with the
萬變歸宗。                          diameter (of the circle), symbolizing extbftian yuan returns
                               to one: the chord of a curve and the diameter of a circle be-
                               come one, curving and straightness are unified, all changes
                               return to the source.

 天元術總論                (Summary of the Celestial Element Method)
 天元術（Tian Yuan Shu，Celestial Element Method） Tian Yuan Shu (Celestial Element Method) is the algebraic
 乃李冶所創之代數方法，為人類歷史上最早之符 method created by Li Ye, one of the earliest symbolic alge-
 號代數學之一。其法以「天元一」（x）為未知數， bras in human history. Its procedure uses “tian yuan one”
 依題意布列多項式，經相消得開方式，最後以正負 (x) as the unknown, constructs polynomials from problem
 開方術求解。                                      conditions, eliminates terms to obtain an equation, and fi-
                                             nally solves by the positive-negative root method.

     天元術之四步 / The Four Steps of Tian Yuan Shu:

   1. 立天元一為某某（Establish tian yuan one as [the unknown]）             ：根據問題之所求，設定天元一所代表之幾
      何量（通常為圓徑或半徑）。
      Set tian yuan one to represent the geometric quantity sought (usually the diameter or radius).

   2. 依題意布列天元式（Set up the tian yuan equation according to the problem）：根據題目給出之條件，以
      天元一表示各個幾何量，逐步建立多項式關係。
      Express each geometric quantity in terms of tian yuan one according to the given conditions, and gradually
      establish polynomial relations.

   3. 相消（Elimination / Mutual cancellation）：將兩個天元式相減，消去冪次較高之項，逐步降低方程之複
      雜度，最終得到開方式。
      Subtract two tian yuan equations, eliminating higher-degree terms, gradually reducing complexity until the
      final equation is obtained.

   4. 開方（Root extraction）     ：以正負開方術（類似霍納法之數值迭代法）求解多項式方程，得到天元一之數
      值。
      Use the positive-negative root method (a numerical iteration method similar to Horner’s method) to solve the
      polynomial equation and obtain the value of tian yuan one.


   天元術之數學形式 / Mathematical Form of Tian Yuan Shu:
   李冶以算籌之位置表示冪次，自上而下為：商                      Li Ye used counting-rod positions to represent pow-
（根）、實（常數項）、方（一次項）     、廉（二次三次 ers, from top to bottom: shang (the root), shi (constant
 項）、隅（最高次項）。                             term), fang (linear term), lian (quadratic and cubic terms),
                                         yu (highest-degree term).


                          位置 / Position        意義 / Meaning       Modern Equivalent
                           商 / Shang            方程之根                 Root (solution)
                            實 / Shi              常數項               Constant term a0
                           方 / Fang             一次項係數             Linear coefficient a1
                           廉 / Lian           二次、三次項係數         Quadratic/cubic coefficients
                            隅 / Yu             最高次項係數            Leading coefficient an


     以現代數學符號表示，天元術解之方程即為：                                   In modern mathematical notation, the equation solved
                                                        by tian yuan shu is:
        an xn + an−1 xn−1 + · · · + a1 x + a0 = 0
                                                                an xn + an−1 xn−1 + · · · + a1 x + a0 = 0

   天元術之歷史意義：李冶之天元術，比歐洲之                                      Historical Significance: Li Ye’s tian yuan shu predates
 符號代數學（以韋達、笛卡兒為代表）早出三百至                                 European symbolic algebra (represented by Vieta and Descartes)
 五百年。其「立天元一」之思想，與現代代數學之                                 by three to five centuries. The idea of “establishing tian
「設 x 為某某」完全一致。天元術之發明，標誌着                                yuan one” is completely identical to modern algebra’s “let
 中國數學從算術向代數之飛躍，為人類數學思想                                  x be [the unknown].” The invention of tian yuan shu marks
 史之重要里程碑。                                               the leap of Chinese mathematics from arithmetic to alge-
                                                        bra, a major milestone in the history of human mathemat-
                                                        ical thought.

後序        (Afterword)


原後序 / Original Afterword
              「吾平生著述，死 When Master Jingzhai (Li Ye) was gravely ill, he said to his
敬齋先生病且革，語其子克修曰：
後可盡燔去。獨測圓海鏡一書，雖九九小數，吾常 son Kexiu: “All my writings may be burned after my death.
精思致力焉，後世必有知者。庶可布廣垂永乎？」 Only the Sea Mirror of Circle Measurements, though it con-
                       cerns but the minor art of arithmetic, has been the object
                       of my deep contemplation and dedicated effort. Posterity
                       will surely have those who understand it. May it thus be
                       spread abroad and transmitted forever?”

  先生於六藝百家，靡不貫串。文集近數百卷，常                               The Master’s mastery extended through the Six Arts
謙謙不自伐。惟於此書不忘稱異。予易簀之間，想                            and the Hundred Schools, without exception. His collected
有玄妙內得於心者。予以先生與先人同榜之故，素                            writings approached several hundred volumes, yet he re-
常兄事克修。克修兄命予重為序。予不敢說論豔                             mained ever modest and self-effacing. Only with this book
藻，刻畫無鹽，唐突西子。直以所聞語之子弟，使                            did he not forget to express his wonder. At the moment of
知測圓海鏡之淵源云。                                        his passing, one imagines he had attained some profound
                                                  mystery within his heart. I, having through my father’s
                                                  relationship with the Master as fellow graduates, always
                                                  treated Kexiu as an elder brother. Elder Brother Kexiu
                                                  commanded me to write a preface anew. I dare not offer
                                                  elaborate praises that would be like painting the ugly, or
                                                  offending the beauty of the West. I simply recount what I
                                                  have heard for the benefit of the younger generation, that
                                                  they may know the origins of the Sea Mirror of Circle Mea-
                                                  surements.
